ParlaMint-AT_2002-04-18-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00101_d7e2895	Please answer me, Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-04-18-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00101_d7e2910	The European Arrest Warrant is an explicit political and legal desire of the EU in a fundamental way. ==References== An accelerated extradition process is being carried out between EU Member States on September 2001, and it means that a so-called simplified, meant an accelerated extradition process. The principle of this new process is that extradition can occur not only in the case of mutual criminality, but also on the basis of certain offences mentioned in a so-called positive list. The condition is that these offences in that State, which desires extradition, are punished by at least three years. Then, after implementing a Framework Decision of December 2001, it may also come to the extradition of its own citizens, theoretically. The Austrian Constitution prohibits the extradition of its own citizens. We have received an additional five-year period for the implementation of this Decision as the only Member State. It is also clear that we will not be extradivided if a procedure against the suppliers has already been carried out in the country. Even if this procedure comes to an end – but that will also be a question of implementation – there will be no extradition of its own citizens, for example; nor in the future.

What is the point of the independent financial Senate, what about the flexibility clause? – Some comments on the establishment of the independent financial Senate. It is the establishment of an independent administrative authority for the whole of the two-in-a-kind legal proceedings, for tax matters, for customs and financial matters without a doubt a very important and very far-reaching matter for many citizens and businesses. The aim is to increase the legal protection standard, which is comparable to the financial jurisdiction in other EU Member States as well as our administrative jurisdiction. Only it cannot be a court, but it must be an independent administrative authority. Citizens' rights are also strengthened by the independence of the judicial agency and by fair and rapid procedures of independent bodies. In the future, the seven financial country directors would focus on the entire two-seconds administrative procedure for tax and customs administration in an independent authority, namely the independent financial Senate. We welcome this path. We firmly believe that this is the right way to achieve something in the medium and long term not only for citizens but also for savings. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] [[Abg. Nuremberger: You haven't won a choice yet! He explains how successful they are!]] Mr Nürnberger, what is this? I am trying here to promote the agreement of the Social Democrats on this real milestone, and Mr Parnigoni wants to interrupt me. Let me continue with all my remarks in peace and fairness. What about the two-in-a-kind appeal procedure at the moment? – On the one hand, there are the independent Senates and on the other hand assigned officials. In addition, the so-called mixed use of these officials, i.e. the officials employed in the operational field, will eventually also take part in the second instance in the appeal proceedings. How does that look in practice? – A tax act is carried out in the unit, the employee is not quite familiar, asks his speaker, the speaker the group leader, the group leader the department head, and the department head ultimately goes to the department head in the second instance in the FLD. Then the decision is made in the sense of what the head of the department has explained in the second instance. The citizen who appeals against this decision, and then the following happens: It is precisely those officials who originally decided how to do the act that decide on this vocation. – This is a legal protection standard that far from meet modern requirements, ladies and gentlemen! This Reform Act and this independent financial Senate would also mean a substantial discharge of the highest courts, the VwGH and the VfGH. Why? – If appeal proceedings are carried out by an independent Senate, the acceptance of the decision in the appeal proceedings is much greater than if a decision is taken by a law-enforced civil servant. Therefore, the number of procedures before the VfGH and before the VwGH will decline significantly. In addition, this independent financial Senate is also in line with the principle of economic efficiency, effectiveness and economy in the administration. This independent financial Senate would not have any restrictions on access to courts. There is no duty to charge, there is no cost in the case of non-observatories, there is no law enforcement. In summary, it can be said that there is a strengthening of citizens' rights, especially because an independent judicial agency is active. How is this now to be implemented in practice? – The head office of the UFS will be Vienna. In the countries of Vorarlberg, Styria, Tirol, Carinthia, Upper Austria and Salzburg, there will be so-called state senates, which, Mr Heindl has already said, summarizes all procedures – including criminal, financial and customs law – in this independent financial Senate. The important and, from my point of view, great in this solution in the form of an independent financial Senate is the fact that the Senate members are pointless, indisputable and irresistible. This means that they can decide freely and are in no way bound by events or service instructions – whether oral or written. They also have no fear of getting a nod in their career planning if they do not focus on what their superior says. I myself have been a member of the Austrian financial administration for about a decade and know what I am talking about and how difficult it is to make a decision as a small speaker or auditor when Mr Hofrat, Mr. Board of Directors or group leader is of different opinion. It is therefore extremely important that independent, inseparable and impermissible Senate members take up their activities. It is also true that the composition of the Senate – two employees from the field and two laymen – results in a balanced relationship in the way of the appeal, but ultimately, it must be decided. The chairman of the Senate has the right to conduct and ultimately decides. Another advantage is that, contrary to the previous legal status, oral proceedings are possible not only before the Senate, but also before the individual official. As a rule, it is intended to be so that, essentially, individual officials first make decisions. In this case too, an oral hearing may be held on the request of the appeal applicant. However, the introduction of this independent financial Senate also takes into account a sustainable and recurring criticism of the Court of Auditors, which repeatedly identifies and criticizes the high number of legal remedies and the long duration of proceedings in the proceedings. This independent financial Senate would make substantial improvements in this respect, namely that it would be faster, more appropriate and more appropriate to the wishes of the recruiter. On the other hand, the financial authority, as such, is not restricted in its rights, because instead of the president's complaint, there will be an official complaint to the financial administration, if it does not agree with the Senate's decision, it would open the way to appeal the highest courts. As part of this independent financial Senate, I still allow myself to make a motion for an amendment by Members Dr. Stummvoll, Böhacker and colleagues in connection with § 300 of the BAO. It is essentially a question of rewording only to clarify that the division of the provisions on entry into force between several paragraphs serves clarity. There are no major changes in content. Ladies and gentlemen! Finally, you allow me to comment briefly on a second point, namely on the Amendment Act 2002, a very thin law, which essentially only brings tax reliefs. It is found that the credit interest paid for tax assets is not subject to income tax or corporation tax, that the date for the entitlement interest of 1. ==References== ==References====External links== There is an improvement in SME financing. With one word, ladies and gentlemen: The government has spoken. There are no new burdens, but – quite the contrary! – new tax reliefs.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-04-18-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00101_d7e6660	Mr President. High house! As has already been pointed out, the independent financial Senate will achieve a significant improvement in the legal system. This serves the taxpayer, it is an improvement for the taxpayer. The independent financial Senate is modelled on the already proven independent administrative senate of the countries, it is re-trained to the independent Bundesasylsenat. At present, in the appeals system, where individual decisions are concerned, we have used partially assigned officials. There is currently a mixture in the subject area and in matters of appeal. If a financial officer is not sure in a decision, he shall call the financial management department and ask: What legal opinion should be used here? – Then the official in question gives a legal opinion, and then the current system may be that the person who has already given a particular direction should decide in the appeal procedure. – Here practically a judge decides on his own case. That is why a new regulation is needed. Moreover, this should not be forgotten, the new regulation brings with it much more individual judge decisions and synergistic effects through reduced Senate. The association of financial academics has welcomed in a letter that this new regulation is coming. Science – Professor Lang, for example, an expert in this field – has welcomed it, the staff representative welcomes it, the union welcomes it. Briefly summarized: What are the benefits for the taxpayer – should our interest actually apply to him? – Rapid treatment through more individual judge decisions, smaller Senates, uniformising the proverbial practice, because there is only one authority with outposts. The harmonisation of proverbial practice is now also achieved in the future through the systematic establishment of a continuous decision-making documentation on a computer-based basis. ==References====External links== In January 2003, we will have to make the fastest decisions, because there are many human, organisational and spatial preparations to be made. Mr Kogler! They argued that the administrative court is not relieved. – That is true, he has pointed out this in the opinion procedure. It concerns § 131 of the Administrative Court Act. We have nothing against the fact that the administrative court is granted a right of rejection – that would certainly come our way! –, the administrative court has only referred to after the trade talks itself: Let us wait for about two years to see how this authority works, whether it really comes to better decisions, and then we will open up the right of rejection! We have responded to this criticism by the Administrative Court, we have talked to President Jabloner about it, and this has come out as a kind agreement. This is not a disadvantage. We would have been able to clarify everything in the conference we had invited. I'm sorry – I was there when the appointment was made – that it didn't come to this conversation. Today, a junkim is produced with the so-called flexibilization clause. Now I would like to ask: What does the establishment of an independent financial Senate have to do with the flexibility clause? – There is no context in terms of content. I would like to explain briefly what the flexibilization clause is all about. The flexibilization clause is about your right of parliamentary approval, ladies and gentlemen. The flexibilisation clause brings a breakthrough, an exception to the principle of intimidation. They adopt an annual federal financial law, and this is an exception here. It also brings about a derogation in the field of speciality. They not only generally approve the expenditure and revenue for a ministry, but also for the individual purposes. There is a derogation from both of them. If this is to be extended to larger areas, then we need to talk about it. Especially here in the house the most detailed treatment should be given. Furthermore, the relationship between the coordination function of the Minister of Finance and the respective department is not balanced at present. Why isn't that balanced? – Because almost no resort has introduced this "flex clause". There are only four areas of service in two departments. Only now, for the sake of the good standing, has it also introduced the Ministry of Finance for the Financial Procuratorship. If this rule were so great – for the affected areas it works well, I admit that already – every department would have torn off. What's the problem with this "flexi clause"? – The departments must continue to comply with all the traditional budgetary rules in their fields and now have a foreign body of competence, which continues to exist in the area of ministerial responsibility. How did that affect? – When there were, for example, savings measures in the course of budgetary consolidation, these flexibilised areas were, of course, excluded according to the principle, and the specialist departments had to continue to accommodate this part in their own budget. These are unacceptable rules. Therefore, a thorough discussion must be held – on the one hand, with regard to parliamentary authorisation rights, on the other hand, there must be a fair balance between the Minister of Finance, who has the technical responsibility and the corresponding budgetary responsibility, and the Minister of Finance, who has the coordination function. If you do not give a constitutional majority to the independent financial Senate today, I would like to invite you to consider what is happening. Then, on a simple legal basis, we have set in motion all the regulations necessary for the establishment of the courts. But we have no independence for the individual judges. We then decide on a goal that cannot work from home. Please remember all these circumstances in your decision-making today! Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-04-18-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00101_d7e7310	Thank you very much, Mr President. Thank you very much, Mr State Secretary! High house! Just as the decision-making on financial market supervision is a milestone in the history of the financial sector, the creation of an independent financial Senate can also be referred to as a milestone. This independent financial Senate, which is to be responsible for the whole of the bi- interest-based legal proceedings for tax matters, customs and financial penalties, constitutes a strengthening of citizens' rights. The independence of the appeal authority will bring about fair and faster procedures before independent bodies. It is also important that there are no restrictions on access, such as the obligation to pay, cost compensation for the case of non-observatories or the obligation to represent. The government proposal was adopted by the Council of Ministers on 19 November. He decided this year and is now scheduled for 1. ==References====External links== So far, in judicial proceedings, decisions have been taken either in non-profit-making states or by individual officials. The members of the new independent financial Senate shall not be bound by any instructions as regards their duties. Furthermore, those members of the independent financial Senate may not exercise any activity for the duration of their duties, which could cause doubt in their independence. The creation of this independent financial Senate not only serves to strengthen citizens' rights but also to bring financial synergies. The Authority is to be housed at the previous locations of the seven financial management departments, in accordance with local circumstances, but geographically separated from the other financial administrations. Staff needs and infrastructure are generally covered by the personnel of the financial country directors. Not least, this independent financial Senate should also relieve the Administrative Court and the Constitutional Court of Justice. For each business unit, the required number of appeals shall be formed within the scope of the business distribution. The legal professional representations shall be sent to each foreign office the required number of members for the appeal institution for a term of six years. Decisions in the appeals centre shall be taken by a simple majority. The General Assembly shall determine the number of members to be sent by the individual professional representatives, taking into account the importance of the professional group for the benefit of the tax. The scope of this new law is reflected in the need for comprehensive changes in the following laws: in the Federal Tax Code, in the Customs Act, in the Procurement Act, in the Introductory Act on Administrative Procedure Laws, in the Financial Criminal Law and in the Tax Administrative Organisation Act. I would like to thank the ladies and gentlemen in the Ministry of Finance for the work done, which brought this new law. Dear ladies and gentlemen! I have already made the point at the outset that the creation of this independent financial Senate is a milestone in the financial sector. I would ask you very politely to give your consent to this important and sensible law.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-04-18-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00101_d7e7976	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! This presentation guarantees the taxpayer – as my previous speakers have already said – that those who decide on his affairs will no longer be exposed to the danger of a legally prescribed " Schizophrenia", which must be taken into account if one has to decide on vocations as a direct individual official. This would now be the end, and its replacement would be independent, non-disputed appeals Senates, which take a decision with the participation of laymen. This legal progress, ladies and gentlemen, would really be welcomed. More recently, the tax law would also include those standards which have long existed in other areas of administration, namely the freedom of guidance and the independence of members of appeals, so that in appeals it would not be a priority for fiscal-friendly and therefore tax-dispute assessments. It is precisely the fact that the financial office as well as the taxpayer is only the same party in the proceedings in the future that will lead to a greater acceptance and objectivity of appeal procedures in the eyes of the legal subjects. The participation of the laymen would also ensure that, in several possible interpretations, the main financial judges do not necessarily choose those who demand the greatest victims to the taxpayer. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! At this point, however, I do not want to hide the fact that the current rules on the participation of the laymen during the review have met with considerable criticism. I do not share this criticism. In my opinion, the accused lack of or insufficient enforcement of the lay people to be sent will strengthen the pressure towards a simpler tax legislation and support the reform efforts in this direction on a sustainable basis. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] Nor do I share the criticism that is based on the only exceptional meaning of the laity vote. According to the draft, their vote will only be decisive if the members of the Senate are in disagreement. However, this regulation, ladies and gentlemen, is not to be criticised, but to welcome it. This regulation would be living democracy. Finally, Mr President, I would like to cordially invite you, ladies and gentlemen of the opposition, to accept this draft, and stress: The legal progress that would be achieved through the scheme would be significant! I hope that this law, which leaves the local competence of the financial Senate open, will not lead to Vorarlberger treating the appeal of Vienna. ParlaMint-AT_2002-04-18-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00101_d7e8054	Mr. State Secretary! Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! This flexibilisation clause was adopted on 3 November 1998, limited to three years. This clause allows departments in ministries, downstream departments of ministries, to take budgetary measures beyond the camera lists and to organise self-management in their departments. As we now know, there has been very successful potential for savings where this model has been temporary for a number of years. This means that the costs in these areas were reduced by up to 18 percent and the independence and decision-making capacity of employees in these departments could also be better developed than in other areas. The commitment of employees has increased. We know that, in this way, it is very possible to put in place sensible savings in many areas of our authorities and our administration, without policy having to try – often very cleverly – great intervention, because Mr X and Mrs Y believe that they have to take great action. Very often, these measures by the ministers have done exactly the opposite. I just remember the savings announced by Mrs Gehrer on a time and again. In the end, these savings measures have always led to cost explosions, as has been shown after the evaluation of the measures. That is, a little more commitment at the official level, a little more confidence in the civil service would allow us to reduce the cost effectively without the policy having to take major measures. Unfortunately, I have to say – this flexibilization clause was implemented at the time with SPÖ and ÖVP votes, but also with the votes of some members of the opposition – the ÖVP in this small coalition, which is currently ruling, has changed its opinion and wants to have this regulation that makes sense again. We do not understand this and therefore call on the ÖVP to put themselves together again in the next few weeks and to allow for a reasonable permanent arrangement here. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-04-18-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00101_d7e13227	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! The discussion at the Austrian universities regarding a university reform law is intense and should at the end of the assessment period cause us to see today the opportunity to discuss the challenges, problems, state and prospects of the Austrian universities. [[President Dr. Fischer is taking over the presidency again.]] In view of the comments made in recent weeks about the Austrian universities, one has to clarify: The Austrian universities do not need to shy international comparisons. They are not perfect, but they have kept themselves extremely good in view of the increased challenges. One may complain that we produce few Nobel Prize winners, but after all international studies we have many excellent graduates, and in a number of scientific disciplines our researchers enjoy world reputation. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and the Greens.]] Our Austrian universities were also unique in a particular area: There is a high level of participation of all university members. There were previously no access barriers such as tuition fees or the Numerus clausus. We have also created new successful ways to allow disadvantaged groups to have access to the university, such as through the introduction of professional maturity exams. As far as the stock is concerned, we have also achieved a world level in some areas, particularly as regards the application of new communication technologies. – Ladies and gentlemen, very much! This is an absolute success balance of three decades of socio-democratic higher education policy! [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] What we are currently experiencing is a conservative turn of higher education policy. Their first advances were the tuition fees, savings programmes, an increasingly depressing service law and in autumn 2001 for the first time since long also declining numbers among students. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! The question is: Will this conservative turn in higher education policy continue, or do we focus on the challenges of the future? – For us, Social Democrats is clear: We do not want the conservative turn, but modern, sustainable universities! [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Despite the good condition of our universities, reforms are needed on the basis of two points: On the one hand, the international environment for our universities is changing. University policy is no longer a national issue alone, but there is also European higher education policy with all its impact on Austria. There is the challenge of the new media, the rise of distance studies, global cooperation and networking, and we also have a resolution of the traditional subject canon, where it is no longer just a study, but where the studies cross into other areas. In other words, it is necessary to address these new challenges, and a reform is therefore needed. A reform is also necessary, because there are still problems in some areas of our higher education system – and this is not to be concealed. The biggest problem is that we still have a small academic share in Austria as a whole and, unfortunately, we are moving towards the end of the scale of the industrialized world. We still have too few women among the university professors, we still have above-average study durations and high drop-out rates, and despite the reform of 1993 we still have elaborate administrative structures with strong dependence on universities from the ministry. We also have a number of structural problems at universities, but one of the biggest disadvantages may be the too little anchorage of universities in the social and regional environment. Funding through third-party funds is also lower in Austria than it would be in line with international standards. Therefore, it makes sense – absolutely meaningful – to discuss a reform of our universities. I therefore support the fact that we are conducting a broad debate on this issue in the Austrian public. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] However, the question arises of how to get a reform. For us, Social Democrats is clear: Reforms require the overwhelming approval of the various interested groups. Who are the groups concerned? – These are the students, the scientists, the administrative staff and ultimately the citizens of our society, the entire society and the economy. Therefore, the different expectations that exist at a university are to bring into a reasonable balance, so that we come to a higher quality. What the different stakeholders expect is not a secret, in terms of teaching, the application of modern information and communication technologies, personal contact. They expect to study free of existential concerns and study, which also creates good opportunities on the national and international labour market. We also want to develop scientific freedom and ultimately create a climate for the freedom of scientific discussion and for the freedom of scientific research. We are not of the opinion, Mrs. Federal Minister, that the freedom of science can be achieved by de-motivating the employees of the Austrian universities. It's not possible! [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and the Greens.]] My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! The Ministry has reviewed a proposal. If you read the various opinions that come from the various groups concerned, and you summarize them, Mrs Federal Minister, I must say that this is not oppositional rhetoric. The opinion of the Austrian Rectors' Conference, which adopted this opinion by a two-thirds majority, reads as follows: It is spoken of the elimination of the self-government character of universities instead of autonomy, the appointment of members of the University Council without university legitimacy, the elimination of the Senate's co-decision on the development and internal organization of the University and of service dictatorship instead of service agreement. It refers to unfair negotiating positions, it is criticized that there is no adequate consideration of the cost of disbursement, that there is foreign determination instead of university self-determination and so on and so on. Mrs. Minister! If you read all these statements – and there are still more of the middle construction and the Austrian high schooling – you can only sum up them with the term "destruction". The assessment of your draft law by all those concerned is an destructive judgment! [[Conjugation with the SPÖ and the Abg. Dr. Grünewald.]] My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! We, the Socialist Group, have not limited ourselves solely to criticizing your draft, but we have been using the last weeks to conduct intensive discussions with the various groups at the universities – with the middle-level construction, with the students, with the professors, with representatives from the economy and with others who are interested in the university. We have drawn up a social-democratic alternative proposal for the reform of Austrian universities – an alternative proposal that will ultimately try to bring together the different interests that exist at universities in a democratic way. That is why we have put a proposal on the table that is based on the fact that there is a modernisation of higher education, and that guarantees a university that is autonomous, democratic, free and efficient. That must be our goal! [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] We have proposed that, involving the different interests of universities, something like an Austrian university conference, in which all institutions of the tertiary sector are represented and in which guidelines are ultimately to be drawn up. We propose a university council that does not exercise party-political influence on universities, but acts as a supervisory body in which the interests of the state, society and the university are summarized. We believe that a university Senate should decide on the development plan and the organisation plan. We also believe that collegiate bodies with decision-making powers should remain below the Senate level. We want qualified individuals in the performance functions of the individual organizations, regardless of whether they are university professors or not. We also want to exercise political responsibility by deciding the principles of the performance agreements and the development plan in the main committee of the National Council, as well as the management of the budget negotiations and the conclusion of the service contracts by the Ministry. We want a truly intense anchoring of universities in Austrian society. That's our goal! [[Conjugation with the SPÖ and the Abg. Dr. Grünewald.]] Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! We have also carried out our proposal in concrete terms through a statute which regulates the overall organisation of a university. There are therefore two proposals for further discussion after the end of the review procedure: There is the proposal of the Ministry, which is based on widespread rejection of all the groups concerned, and there is the proposal of the Social Democratic Parliamentary Group, which covers the concerns of the students up to the professors and presents a future-oriented concept for the university organisation. Our reform concept focuses on co-determination, democratic self-management and high quality in teaching and research. Mrs. Federal Minister! You should join him! [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] The SPÖ concept integrates the interests of society and all those involved in higher education into the reform. It takes into account the concerns and concrete proposals of the professors, middle-level construction and students. The democratic responsibility and co-determination of all university members remain. Self-government structures are being strengthened, party-political influence is being reduced, genuine autonomy is the goal of our reform, ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] [[Abg. Dr. Grollitsch: Why didn't you...?]] We maintain a high quality in teaching and research, we also want to develop it forward-looking. We want to receive the existing service offer for students and abolish existing access restrictions such as the tuition fees at the earliest possible time. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] We make a proposal that will continue to offer career prospects for young scientists at the Austrian universities in the future and therefore will not let universities compete with the private economy in terms of qualified employees. There is a competition between good private sector offers and worse working conditions that you offer at universities. If you want our students to be able to work with highly qualified scientific staff, there must also be comparable, acceptable and competitive working conditions at the universities for teachers there. This is also a goal of ours, ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen. [[New applause at the SPÖ.]] It is significant – and this has also happened in other countries in university debates – that the more quality is reduced on the one hand, the more the resources are restricted, the more the propagandaist vocabulary becomes on the other hand. The more restricted, the more often terms such as "excellent" and "world union" are used and so on. My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! This has already been heard from other countries. It is not possible to achieve a higher quality at universities with increasingly less and less resources. I think we should also be realistic: If you're talking about world peaks, you have to say what the world's top looks like. When leading the American model with 3,500 universities and colleges, it must be borne in mind that a maximum of 30 of them have an international level and the rest cannot keep up with our universities. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! We must ensure that we – and this must be our goal – have a well-qualified academic potential, and ensure that the academic rate in Austria increases if we want to be internationally competitive. That must be the goal for business, society and universities! [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] The university reform according to the Ministry's proposal has been widely rejected by all concerned. Our concept, which we presented last Monday, has already caused initial reactions. I would like to read to you what the former rector of the University of Vienna wrote to us. He wrote: Ladies and gentlemen! I read the SPÖ draft for the Uni reform. I wanted to congratulate you on this. This has become a very good paper, and it has been built on it. - Quote. [[Abg. Ing. This is included in our draft. I hope you can at least accept this proposal. The culture of cooperation is also reflected in the example of medical universities or faculties. You need special provisions. That was, I think, all right. You have now put a solution proposal on the table, and we would like to be here today in the National Council, Mrs. Federal Minister, that you think it possible for universities to decide on it and to receive medical faculties as faculties. This is a broad desire at all the universities concerned, because the medicine lives on cooperation. Medicine is not only the doctor, but it includes computer science, ethics, psychology, chemistry and natural sciences. I can quote to you what Professor Margreiter, the Innsbruck Transplant Professor with World Call, wrote: "Of course, you could distinguish the structures a little bit more clearly" and make your own universities. "But this is bought with a wealth of disadvantages: Teurer will probably do it, and the scientific cooperation and the staff will lose us. Austria... continues to fall back." If you are talking about "world class", you should also take seriously what people tell you who really have an international reputation and experience with medical universities and advise you against it. Last point: the political bandage. There is no doubt that this university council is a political link. [[Abg. Dr. Brinek: Why?]] We want to take in the interests of society... – Because this government sends its trusted representatives there. [[Abg. Dr. Brinek: Why?]] This has never been the case, that of five people in the top management structure, two or even three were sent from their own government lines. [[Abg. Dr. Brinek: Two!]] This is miles away from what you can understand by autonomy. We stand for a reform that should be well thought out for modern, high-performance universities. However, we also stand for open access, for an appreciation of the employees at the universities. We stand for co-determination, for autonomy without party influence. For us, there is no reform against the universities, but only a reform together with the universities!

ParlaMint-AT_2002-04-18-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00101_d7e17175	Mr President. High house! Ladies and gentlemen of the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats) and European Democrats! I can't serve you with the one minute because some things have to be discussed and said. [[Abg. May. Schweitzer introduces himself to the speaker and considers a sign that he has attached to his sacco.]] [[The speaker takes the sign in his hand and keeps it in the direction of the Abg. May. Schweitzer.]] - Should I stop you a little? [[The speaker put the label on the speaker's desk.]] You can look at it in the meantime, Karl! My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! In this context, we have to discuss a number of things that do not have to do with the current issue of immunity, but rather how Parliament, as a collegial body, has dealt with questions of immunity in the past and will deal with them in the future. I should like to remind you that in 1996, on the basis of various events, the House decided by a majority to protect citizens in the exercise of their rights and in the exercise of their dignity before politicians who condone them, belittle them, or act against them in a way that is harmful to credit, in such cases, in spite of the political context, the extradition of the relevant Member will take place. This regulation has led to a reduction in the number of cases and that even those who were members of the House and who in the years before have been vehemently and increasingly strong and increasingly often damaged citizens in their dignity have been extradited. The number of such cases has therefore been reduced, and this decision-making practice of the Immunities Committee has also proved successful until the vote that was held here in the House a few weeks ago, where the Immunities Committee had agreed to extradition, but most of the political groups of the EIA and the Liberals have not followed their club members and have not agreed to the extradition of a Member of this House. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! The previous decision-making practice of the Immunities Committee has therefore failed, since the right of immunity is a collegiate right of this House, it cannot be decided by a majority whether Members may continue to devalue citizens, display credit-damaging behaviour and not be extradited because they belong to the majority group, whereas others who do not belong to the majority group are extradited for the same offences if they commit them. Instead, immunity law must be a collegiate and easy-to-use protection right for Members, to whom we also confess. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Ladies and gentlemen! This right of protection must also apply to the representatives and representatives of minorities in this House. I therefore believe that a step has been taken at a meeting of the Immunities Committee, although there have been repeated announcements by the government groups that a new system in the field of immunity, which would also include the civil protection of this immunity instrument, would be sought and negotiated accordingly. These negotiations have not yet taken place. Although excellent documents have been prepared, the negotiations have not taken place, which is why we are in the situation today, that the National Council in the field of immunity is returning to the regulation before 1996 and that, although it is difficult for me to do so, some Members here in the House are likely to take advantage of it again, which is actually a protection mechanism of the collegial body, namely parliamentary immunity, in order to treat politically active citizens in a form that is credit-damaging and deplorable. I'm afraid so. That is why I refer to the current reluctance to this decision-making practice as a step back. Ladies and gentlemen! I hope that, as is announced in the passus, which is linked to the decision of the Immunities Committee, we will actually enter into a comprehensive discussion of the concept of immunity and a decision-making practice at the beginning of the next legislative period, which will also include a legislative amendment on the right of immunity for Members of this House. I hope that these negotiations will be effective and that we will have a good settlement, which, on the one hand, will be able to ask the right of the parliamentarian to ask unpleasant questions, without being threatened in his existence, and which, on the other hand, will also protect citizens from violations of some of the parliamentarians, as was the case in the past. I hope we get to it. At the moment, we have taken a step back. But in order to maintain the collegiate right, we will also act accordingly and will not deliver Mr Westenthaler, Mr Gaugg. [[Abg. Gaugg: That's right!]] [[Abg. Gaugg: That's right!]]

We have changed the saying practice in 1996. It was evaluated in 1998 and the procedure was confirmed. We had 11th. In May of last year, there was a very intense debate about this because Mr Pilz wanted an extradition. This has not been done because it cannot be decided in accordance with or, so to speak, from the occasion of an individual. There is no doubt that this should not be the decision-making possibility of a single person, but it is a decision-making competence of the collegiate body. Ladies and gentlemen! It is important to me, as the chairman of the Immunities Committee, that decisions are not taken out of the mood of day and not in accordance with the aspect of "government against opposition", but actually in a quiet, fair, objective debate. I do not agree to confirm what Mr Gradwelt has said: that we have decided that we should talk about a fundamental reform. But all the talks in this respect are obviously not so far off, that we could come to such a decision today. However, we have set out to return to the decision-making practice before 1996, and have also established – by the way, unanimously – that this decision-making practice should be valid until the end of this legislative period. Then let us take another look at the decisions made. Ladies and gentlemen! This principle decision, which is right, has at present led to a reduction in the so-called extradition demands. But that was only a short time. In the so far short period of XXI. The legislative period has already been 25 requests for extradition. So we got back to that number as it had been given earlier than was clearly spoken under the protection of immunity more clearly – I deliberately formulate it like that. Ladies and gentlemen! My appeal to everyone is: No one prevents a parliamentarian from arguing in such a way, despite immunity, that the public has a real picture of colleagues. No one prevents a parliamentarian from presenting formulations and arguments in such a way that there is no reason for extradition. I ask everyone to take this note seriously. In particular, we should be aware that from the point of view of the outsiders and those who are not here, immunity is always seen as a privilege. I mean, she's not a privilege. It is an important achievement so that political agents can speak according to abuses and matters. We should always strive to do so objectively.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-04-18-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00101_d7e17417	The next speaker was Mrs Petrovic. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-04-18-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00101_d7e17439	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! I give to the honourable Member and the chairman of the committee It is right that it is important in terms of immunity to have a predictable, calculable line. I have not really approved the previous line of the Immunities Committee after 1996, because on the one hand I was not aware of the principle that certain paragraphs had been made possible for delivery despite a clear political context. Above all, I could not see why you're doing that. I firmly believe that the ordinary courts cannot guarantee the protection of citizens who may be violated by a representative or by a mandate in their rights. I have long suggested that we not only reflect on the question of civil law and the economic power of some companies or companies that do not like or feel affected by certain political statements. I also said that especially for so-called small people or for people who are not in the public, the judgment of a court that takes place three, four or five years later is too late to make up for a violation of honor or reputation that occurred at all times. You will give me the right to admit that in many cases, when years have passed, the attack of a politician or a politician was unjust, and in which serious personal accusations – I think you also know such cases – did not really justify the damage that occurred a year later. That is why I said – and I was very pleased that President Fischer has now proposed this, in view of this really unfrozen and bad book on the President of the Federal Republic of Germany and his family – that another instrument should be set up here, which in such cases can express a more rapid and efficient rejection. But I am, as I have said, at least once satisfied with the fact that we return to the old saying practice. I hope that the reform debate on civil immunity and faster response to violations by well-known personalities will not fall asleep and that we will continue here. It is just as clear that there is a free mandate in this direction, and so my colleague Pilz will subsequently express his position here. For me – and I think that for the majority of the green club – it is important to have a principle that was introduced at the time for very, very good reasons, namely to make clear the priority of legislation over other state functions – in particular execution – and above all to ensure the ability of the executive to act before any administrative shock. To that extent, I am delighted about this return to the old proverbial practice and I am happy to agree with it. For me, always the principle and the safeguarding of the principle of immunity was more important than perhaps occasionally my certain anger about certain statements made by Mr Ing. Westenthaler.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-04-18-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00101_d7e17505	Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! To date, no one has convinced me that Members of the National Council should have the right to punish people in this Republic without penalty. Immunity always had a different meaning. Immunity was never thought of as a privilege of a single Member. Immunity was always thought of as a legal guarantee that the National Council may, under all circumstances, meet in its chosen composition. A procedure according to § 111 certainly does not threaten the meeting of the National Council in its chosen form. If Mr. Ing. Westenthaler once again a journalist, a political opponent, an artist, simply insulted and insulted a dissident and stood in front of an ordinary court, he will nevertheless be prevented from exercising his National Council mandate by no one. [[Abg. Dr. Martin Graf: He never does!]] [[Abg. Dr. Martin Graf: It's always just you!]] [[Intrusion of the Abg. Ing. Shame.]] Why should he, therefore, why should we and why should each of us be protected from ill-reputed proceedings? Then there is another problem, and I would also like to remind you of this problem. It is still possible to complain not only to Members but also to all people in this Republic in such a way as to make a personal decision as to whether one can sit down to do so. A civil action under § 1330 ABGB in conjunction with a complaint of finding can – and I can tell you about it authentically – end up with a dispute value of 100 million schilling. Here there is an incredible inequality before the law and before the judge or judge. [[Abg. Neudeck: And the damage...?]] I appeal to you, ladies and gentlemen, to close these gaps, not only for Members of this House, but for all the people who may be affected by such procedures. If there is a dispute, then the possession and assets should not decide whether to put an end to a civil judge or a civil judge. That's why: Introducing the limitation of the dispute and also fixing the ceiling of 270 000 S by law in the case of the finding! These are tasks of the National Council, if he thinks not only of himself and his members, but of: How should equal opportunities be created before the Court of Justice for all people in this Republic? Ladies and gentlemen! To discuss this, I recommend that we make a proposal from the green club – there will be no differences with us – in the near future: How can we jointly enforce this equal opportunities in civil law for all the people of this Republic in the National Council? On the issue of criminal immunity protection, I would like to say: Bad renunciation is certainly not a crime for which there could and should be free letters. I am sure – and experience shows it – that an overwhelming majority of the members of this House would never make use of such free letters. However, I know at least one Member who would completely misunderstand this free letter. For this reason alone, I am in favour of not making evil intercession the subject of parliamentary immunity and of handing over the Westenthaler to the court which he really deserved according to all his powers. Thank you.
